# APX Next CPQ Rule Catalog — extracted from `APX_Next_config.xml`

Source: `APX_Next_config.xml` (BigMachines/Oracle CPQ export, `bm_config_zip_cache`), the raw source of the `ApxNextConfig` catalog already known to `src/aryx/cpq/*`. Classification logic here mirrors `CpqEngine._load_value_rules` / `load_hiding_rules` / `_resolve_targets` exactly — rule_type is trusted ONLY for the hiding split (`rule_type==11`, the one code confirmed stable across ingested catalogs); every other rule is classified by each action's `set_type` (`-1` = constraint/exclude, anything else = recommendation/assign), never by `rule_type`.

## Summary

| Metric | Count |
|---|---|
| Config attributes (`bm_config_attr`) | 427 |
| Rules total (`bm_config_rule`) | 688 |
| BML functions (`bm_function`) | 923 |
| Hiding-rule target entries | 456 |
| Constraint-rule entries (declarative + script) | 208 |
| Recommendation-rule entries (declarative) | 157 |
| **Script-backed recommendation entries — NOT currently evaluated** | **444** |
| Ambiguous multi-value recommendations skipped | 21 |
| Rules with an unresolvable target | 1 |
| Rules whose top-level condition itself is a script | 173 |
| Rules with more than one condition input (AND-candidates) | 445 |

## Key findings / gaps observed in THIS export

1. **444 script-backed recommendation actions are extracted but never evaluated.** `apply_recommendation_rules()` has no BML-evaluator hookup (unlike `apply_constraint_rules()`, which does via `BmlEvaluator.allowed_values_for_script`). These rules ARE present and readable in this file — they are simply not wired to a `RecommendationRule.script` field today. See `script_recommendations_skipped` below.

2. **445 of 688 rules (65%) carry more than one `bm_config_rule_input` row**, i.e. their real condition is a multi-attribute AND (or an OR-list, depending on `rule_input_index`/operator semantics), but `CpqEngine._load_rule_join_data` keeps only the LAST input row per rule id (`inputs_by_rule[rid] = (aid, val)` overwrites). Every declarative rule below with `all_inputs_count > 1` is losing condition attributes in the current loader.

3. **`bm_config_rule_input_action` (955 rows) is parsed nowhere in `rdb.py`/`engine.py`.** It links a rule to attributes referenced inside an action's formula/script — likely additional *independent* variables a script-backed action depends on beyond its own literal comparisons.

4. **1 rule(s)** have no resolvable target via action / marked-attr / chain and are excluded entirely (same "never silently guess" behavior as the live engine).

# Gap Deep-Dive & Impact Analysis

Addendum to the catalog above -- quantifies the blast radius of the two gaps found in `_load_rule_join_data` / `_load_value_rules`, computed directly from the raw XML (not the RDB), so these numbers reflect the source export, not any prior ingestion run.

## Gap A -- multi-input (AND/OR) conditions collapsed to the last row

| Input rows per rule | Rule count |
|---|---|
| 0 | 56 |
| 1 | 187 |
| 2 | 116 |
| 3 | 191 |
| 4 | 75 |
| 5 | 29 |
| 6 | 16 |
| 7 | 4 |
| 8 | 7 |
| 9 | 3 |
| 10 | 1 |
| 12 | 2 |
| 16 | 1 |

- **442 of 445** multi-input rules reference 2+ different attributes (a real cross-attribute AND) -- only 3 are same-attribute repeats. Several rules mix BOTH: the same attribute repeated with different values (an OR-list, e.g. ENHANCEMENT LEVEL 1 / ENHANCEMENT LEVEL 2 / MULTI-CODE PLUG PROGRAMMING on one attribute) plus other distinct attributes ANDed in -- a genuine boolean expression, not a simple range. No row anywhere uses operator2/value2 (0 rows total), and operator1 (codes 1/2/3/4/5/7/8, dominated by 4 and 3) is never consulted by the live declarative evaluator -- it only ever does `current_val.lower() == condition_value.lower()`.

- **101 of 348 declarative rules (29%) are structurally dead today**: the KEPT (last) input row has an EMPTY value1. `_filled_by_rule_id` only ever contains attrs that have a real filled value, so a condition that means "attribute X is blank" can never be matched -- the entire rule is inert, not just missing an AND clause.
  - Sample dead rules: 'Set Priviledged User Group', 'Hide Continue Buttons When UDCC is blank', 'Setting the config attribute value based on commerce', 'Set UDCC Flag for Non-Privileged users', 'Set Price Admin Package Choices', 'Import Dynamic CSS file for Package Build', 'Default the dynamic CSS to hide Navigation Menu Based on User Group', 'Restrict Product Selection Attribute For Series Model'

- **119 rules fire on their kept clause but silently drop a second, real (non-empty) condition** -- these do not fail closed, they fail OPEN: they restrict/hide/recommend based on only part of their intended condition, which reads as unexplained over-triggering in production today, not an absence of behavior.

- Top condition attributes silently discarded (not the one kept):

| Attribute (dropped as a condition) | Rules affected |
|---|---|
| modelSelectionbaseModel_astro | 221 |
| hiddenMasterStringForAstroPortable_astro | 90 |
| hidddenRecordSeparator_allFamilly | 73 |
| productSelectionProduct_all | 61 |
| _bm_model_variable_name | 58 |
| ultimateDestinationCountry | 53 |
| softwareBundlesBundleType_astro | 50 |
| customerType | 38 |
| additionalApplicationServices_astro | 37 |
| packageTypeBundles_astro | 35 |
| hiddenConstraintMasterString_astro | 24 |
| packageInvokedFlag | 22 |
| sME_EBSFlag | 20 |
| bulkAddFlag | 20 |
| customerUIN | 19 |

**Why this matters**: the most-dropped attributes are the catalog's own central gates -- base model, product line, destination country, customer type. Rules meant to read "if model=X AND country=Y AND customerType=Z" are today evaluating on whichever of those three happened to be inserted last -- a shadow-mode diff should specifically exercise model-selection, country/FEDERAL, and package/promotion paths, since that is where behavior will visibly change.

## Gap B -- script-backed recommendations extracted but never evaluated

- 444 action-entries, across **127 distinct rules**, targeting **200 unique attributes** (141 customer-visible, 59 hidden/backend-only).

- **None target a required=True attribute** -- closing this gap does not unblock any currently-stuck required question; the risk/value profile is commerce and UX quality, not quote-completion blocking.

- **Every one of the 444 entries is backed by its own distinct BML function id** (444 distinct functions, zero sharing) -- there is no small shared core to port once and reuse.

- **Feasibility reality check**: scanning each script's first 300 characters for Tier-1 blockers (for/while/dict(/recordset(/util./split() and the returnVal= idiom Tier-1 requires -- only about 6 of 444 look like simple if/else value-pickers; the remaining ~438 are small procedural programs (delimited-string building, dictionary/recordset manipulation, util.constants() lookups) that the existing Tier-1 mini-evaluator structurally cannot parse. Adding a script hookup like constraints have only closes a handful of these directly -- the rest need either Tier-2 LLM evaluation (non-deterministic, and bml_use_llm is off by default for cost/latency reasons) or hand-ported Python equivalents for the highest-value cases.

- Highest-frequency targets (best candidates to hand-port first, since they recur across many rules and touch commerce logic -- Promotion Table, commerce-attribute propagation):

| Target attribute | Rules referencing it |
|---|---|
| systemEnhancementFeatureType_astro | 9 |
| serviceDuration_astro | 9 |
| beltClipType_astro | 9 |
| operationModeType_astro | 8 |
| serviceType_astro | 8 |
| packingPackageType_astro | 8 |
| multikeyType_astro | 8 |
| productSelectionProduct_all | 7 |
| modelSelectionmadeInAmerica | 7 |
| antennasType_astro | 7 |
| modelSelectionSubmersibleDeltaT_astro | 7 |
| modelSelectionHazardousLocation_astro | 7 |
| batteryType_astro | 7 |
| manual_astro | 7 |
| advancedSystemKeyHardwareKey_astro | 7 |

## Net impact if both gaps are closed

- Gap A fix reaches **220 of 348 declarative rules with any input (63%)** -- 101 currently-dead rules would start firing, 119 currently-over-firing rules would become correctly gated. This is a correctness fix to the MAJORITY of the declarative rule set, not an edge case -- treat it as a behavior change requiring the shadow-mode regression diff already recommended, prioritized on model / country / customerType / package paths.

- Gap B fix is real but lower-leverage and higher-effort per rule: 200 attributes affected but zero required, and ~99% of the scripts need more than a simple hookup. Recommend scoping phase 1 to the top ~15 highest-frequency targets above (hand-ported) rather than attempting all 444 at once.
